=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N cells in a row, numbered 1 to N.
For each 1 \leq i < N, cells i and i+1 are adjacent.
Initially, cell i is painted with color i.
You are given Q queries. Process them in order. Each query is of one of the following two types.

- 1 x c: Repaint the following to color c: all reachable cells reachable from cell x by repeatedly moving to an adjacent cell painted in the same color as the current cell.
- 2 c: Print the number of cells painted with color c.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N Q
\mathrm{query}_1
\vdots
\mathrm{query}_Q

Each query is given in one of the following two formats:
1 x c

2 c

Output

Let q be the number of queries of the second type. Print q lines.
The i-th line should contain the answer to the i-th such query.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 5 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq Q \leq 2 \times 10^5
- In queries of the first type, 1 \leq x \leq N.
- In queries of the first and second types, 1 \leq c \leq N.
- There is at least one query of the second type.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 6
1 5 4
1 4 2
2 2
1 3 2
1 2 3
2 3

Sample Output 1

3
4

The queries recolor the cells as shown in the figure.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

DSU parent array of size N+1. `find(x)`:
def find(x):
    while parent[x] != x:
        parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
        x = parent[x]
    return x
This path halving. Since parent values always smaller (deleted points to previous alive), no cycles. Good.

Let's think about DSU deletion correctness more formally:
We maintain parent array where for alive i, parent[i]=i. For deleted i, parent[i] = some j < i (eventually find(i-1)). find(i) follows parent pointers to an alive root. Because parent pointers always decrease, no cycles. The root is the largest alive index <= i? Need prove by induction. Initially true. When deleting i, set parent[i]=find(i-1), which is largest alive <= i-1. For any j < i, unaffected. For j > i, if their find previously returned i? Could that happen? If j is a deleted start whose parent chain led to i (because i was alive and largest alive <= j). After deleting i, find(j) will follow chain to i then to find(i-1), returning largest alive <= i-1, which is also largest alive <= j (since no alive between i and j? Wait if j > i and i was the largest alive <= j, then after deleting i, largest alive <= j is find(i-1). So correct). For j = i, find(i) returns find(i-1). For j < i, unaffected. Thus find(x) returns largest alive <= x. Good.

Let's write code:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    it = iter(data)
    N = next(it); Q = next(it)
    parent = list(range(N + 1))
    R = list(range(N + 1))
    col = list(range(N + 1))
    cnt = [0] + [1] * N

    def find(x):
        while parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
            x = parent[x]
        return x

    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        t = next(it)
        if t == 1:
            x = next(it); c = next(it)
            s = find(x)
            if col[s] == c:
                continue
            old = col[s]
            r = R[s]
            length = r - s + 1
            cnt[old] -= length
            cnt[c] += length
            col[s] = c

            if s > 1:
                l = find(s - 1)
                if col[l] == c:
                    R[l] = r
                    parent[s] = l
                    s = l

            r = R[s]
            if r < N:
                right = find(r + 1)
                if col[right] == c:
                    R[s] = R[right]
                    parent[right] = s
        else:
            c = next(it)
            out.append(str(cnt[c]))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's maybe implement find with full path compression (recursive or iterative two-pass) to improve. Path halving is okay. But if we want stronger amortized, we can do iterative full compression:
def find(x):
    p = parent
    root = x
    while p[root] != root:
        root = p[root]
    while p[x] != x:
        nxt = p[x]
        p[x] = root
        x = nxt
    return root
This compresses all nodes on path to root. More operations per find but better. Path halving simpler and fast. Since parent pointers decrease, path halving also compresses. Use path halving.

But wait, I need ensure code uses `parent.copy()` for R and col? Let's decide final code. Use `parent = list(range(N + 1))`, `R = parent.copy()`, `col = parent.copy()`. Is there any risk? Let's test with sample mentally. It works. But if `parent.copy()` shares int objects, and later `col = parent.copy()`, col[0]=0. Good.

However, there is a subtle issue: `parent.copy()` for R and col means R and col initially reference the same int objects as parent. But when we do `col[s] = c`, `c` is an int from input. If c is a small int, okay. If c is large, it's an int object from map iterator. Later, if we do `parent[s] = l`, parent[s] references int object l. col[s] remains c. No issue. But what about `R[l] = r` where `r` is an int object from R[s] or R[right]. If `r` is the same int object as some parent value? no issue. Good.

But there is a possible issue: `R = parent.copy()` and `col = parent.copy()` share int objects with parent. If we later mutate `parent` list, it doesn't mutate int objects. Good.

Let's think if using `parent.copy()` for R and col is okay when N=0? N>=1. Good.

Now, code with `it = map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split())`. This map object holds bytes list. Fine.

Let's write final code:

import sys

def main():
    it = map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split())
    N = next(it)
    Q = next(it)

    parent = list(range(N + 1))
    R = parent.copy()
    col = parent.copy()
    cnt = [0] + [1] * N

    def find(x):
        p = parent
        while p[x] != x:
            p[x] = p[p[x]]
            x = p[x]
        return x

    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        t = next(it)
        if t == 1:
            x = next(it)
            c = next(it)
            s = find(x)
            if col[s] == c:
                continue
            old = col[s]
            r = R[s]
            length = r - s + 1
            cnt[old] -= length
            cnt[c] += length
            col[s] = c

            if s > 1:
                l = find(s - 1)
                if col[l] == c:
                    R[l] = r
                    parent[s] = l
                    s = l

            r = R[s]
            if r < N:
                right = find(r + 1)
                if col[right] == c:
                    R[s] = R[right]
                    parent[right] = s
        else:
            c = next(it)
            out.append(str(cnt[c]))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
